    - **`:772` "Respond"** — the file's most tempting outlier, sitting directly beside a real `<Button>`, and the one I most expected to migrate. It stays raw for a reason **already recorded in the code**: it carries a *dynamic per-kind solid fill* (`danger`/`warn`/`ok`/`info`) and `Button`'s solid variants are `primary` and `danger` only. Migrating means either inventing `warn`/`info` solid variants (a token/variant-set redesign) or splitting one control across two spellings by kind. Left alone.
    - **`:1378` view tab** — a tab bar item with an `on` state **and a live pending-change count badge**. `ui/Segmented` is the primitive, but it takes a declarative `options` array with no per-option child slot, so this is a structural rewrite of the tab bar, not a control swap.
    - **Disclosure triggers (3):** `:1616` (full-width uppercase section header with a rotating chevron), `:3151` (inline chevron + "evidence"), and the `aria-expanded` pattern generally — the system ships no disclosure-trigger primitive, and both are neutral-on-neutral, so `TextLink` (coral) is the wrong target.
    - **`:2799` dirty-tab close** — its glyph is a **CSS-hover-swapped pair** (a dot that hides on `group-hover`, an X that appears). `IconButton` takes one `icon`; expressing this needs either a new prop for one site or converting the swap to JS hover state, i.e. a behaviour rewrite. Its clean-tab twin (`:2809`) migrated; this one is honestly harder and stayed.
    - **Why the neutral→coral family stayed raw (`:925`, `:1736`, `:3378`):** these are "neutral label that goes coral on hover", and a tree-wide count found **21 sites across 11 files** using that idiom. It is the app's, not a cockpit outlier — so converting three of them to always-coral `TextLink` would have made *them* the outliers and rewritten the majority idiom on one page. The two I did migrate (`:1913`/`:3389`) moved because they are a *named* system pattern ("Show more"), not merely because they hover coral.
- **The `LoadError` rail takes its FIFTH vocabulary widening — and the rail asked for it in writing.** `ui/loadErrorState.test.tsx` requires each adopter to have a loading state, matched as `<ListSkeleton|<Loading|<FormSkeleton`; the cockpit's page gate is a centred `<Loader2 className="animate-spin">` and matched none. The rail's own comment says "**When a rail rejects a new adopter, check the rail before the adopter**" and "the vocabulary is what widens; the property being checked does not", so `<Loader2` joined the set rather than the page being rewritten — there is **no spinner primitive** to send it to (`Loading` is a bare "Loading…" text line whose doc calls itself "THE LESSER IDIOM AND STAYS SO"), and swapping a full-page spinner for text to satisfy a regex is the inversion the rail warns against. **Verified non-weakening before widening:** every prior adopter that uses `<Loader2` at all uses it *after* its `<LoadError>` (projects 146/449, code 326/367, apps 563/830), so the `Math.min` does not move for any of the thirteen.
- **Two blind spots in that rail, recorded at the call site rather than papered over.** (1) The capture matcher is **file-scoped**, and this file independently satisfies it via an unrelated `.catch((e) => { if (alive) setErr(…) })` (the terminal-session error, ~:2990) — so for this adopter the check passes *for the wrong reason*. The real capture is `setLoadErr` inside `load()`, and it is genuine, but the rail cannot tell them apart. (2) `cachedCalls` extracts only **single-quoted** cache keys, so this file's one `useCachedData` — `` `code:project:${id}` ``, a template literal — is invisible to both swallow checks. That call **does** `.catch(() => null)`, and deliberately: it is an instant-paint seed (`persist:false`) that only ever *sets* `project` when it has data, so a failed seed cannot mask `loadErr`. Left as-is rather than widening the key regex, which would flag a correct swallow as a defect. Both written into the `ADOPTERS` comment where the next adopter will read them.
- **`cappedListDisclosed.test.tsx` pinned `+{hidden} more` as a literal `</button>`** under the heading "an EXPANDING control is not a residue line — left alone deliberately". Read closely, that rail protects **interactivity** (don't converge an actionable disclosure onto a static "+4 more" residue row), not the element spelling — and `TextLink` renders a `<button type="button">`, so the control it guards is unchanged. Matcher updated to the new tag **plus** an assertion that the `onClick` still expands the list, so the check is about the disclosure rather than the tag. Flagging it as the one place a reader might reasonably overrule me; it is a two-line revert.
- **Ratchet moved in the same commit: `rawButton` 272 → 265** (`design/primitiveAdoption.baseline.json`) — 272 was exact before, and 265 is the exact live count after the 7 migrations. `docs/design/consistency-audit.json` regenerated by the suite agrees independently: `pages/code/CodeCockpitPage.tsx` **21 → 14 raw-button**, dropping it from the **#1** ranked drift offender (score 46) to **#3** (score 32). `rawInput` (4 in this file) untouched — out of scope.
- **Falsification (each mutation applied to the live line, red observed, restored from a file copy — never `git checkout`).** (a) Dropped `<LoadError>` + its import from the cockpit → `loadErrorState` red: *"must render the shared primitive: expected … to match /<LoadError\b/"*, with `tsc` still clean, proving the mutation was valid TS and the rail — not the compiler — caught it. (b) Removed `/<Loader2\b/` from the widened vocabulary → red: *"the surface must have a loading state at all: expected Infinity to be less than Infinity"*, proving the widening is load-bearing and not a vacuous addition. (c) Re-added one raw `<button>` → `primitiveAdoption` red: *"New bespoke `<button>`(s) detected (266 > 265)"*, proving the ratcheted baseline bites at exactly the new floor. The other two rails falsified themselves: `accentChip`'s staleness check and `cappedListDisclosed`'s matcher both went red **on their own** the moment the migrations landed, before I touched them — which is the strongest evidence in this cycle that the two controls I converted were the two the system had already flagged.
- **Gate (full, from the repo root, venv on PATH).** `npm run typecheck --workspace web` clean (`tsc --noEmit` + `tsconfig.sw.json`); `npm test --workspace web` — the **FULL** suite, **380 files / 3873 tests, 0 failures** (a path-scoped run would have missed `accentChip` and `cappedListDisclosed` entirely — both were only visible in the whole-suite run, and both were red); `npm run build --workspace web` clean (the >500 kB chunk warning is pre-existing); `make lint` exit 0 (black 1771 files, isort, flake8, mypy 909 sources). No Python source touched, so no `pytest` leg beyond that. `search_arcc` unavailable in this environment — noted, not silently skipped.
- **Live validation — driven in a real browser, in BOTH themes, and cy17's theme caveat is now retired.** Served this worktree's frontend on Vite `:3100` proxied to a gateway on `:10000` with an ISOLATED `PERSONALCLAW_HOME` (deleted after) and `AUTH_MODE=none`, then drove it with the bundled Playwright + Chromium. cy17 had to record that its two "light" captures rendered identical to dark because it toggled a `documentElement` class; the theme is a **`mode` key in localStorage**, set via `addInitScript`, and the two captures are genuinely different palettes (dark `body` `rgb(15,15,15)` / danger icon `rgb(246,108,102)`; light `rgb(240,244,248)` / `rgb(175,47,41)`, and the header's moon becomes a sun).
  - **A mounted-ness floor first, because the naive drive lied.** A fresh dev home **hijacks every route to `#/onboarding`**, so the first run reported "0 role=alert, stale copy gone: true" — a clean-looking result that was really a page that never rendered. Skipped onboarding, then asserted the cockpit shell ("All projects") was present before believing any measurement.
  - **Clause 1a, both themes, with `GET /api/loops/:id` forced to 500** (not 404/400, which the page routes to its permanent `'missing'` state instead of `loadErr`): `role="alert"` present, `<h2>` **"Couldn't load your project"**, **the server's own message rendered on screen** — which is the specific proof that `loadErr` now carries the rejection rather than a flattened string — and one Retry that **re-fetches when clicked** (so the control is live, not decorative). The second `role="alert"` on the page is a pre-existing empty `sr-only` shell region, not a duplicate from this change.
  - **Two of the seven migrations driven live** (`:2786`/`:2816`, by collapsing the Files and Tasks panels on a real project created through `POST /api/loops`): both reopen chips measure `border-radius 9999px`, `height 28px`, `font-size 13px`, ink `text-on-surface`, a ghost hover background that paints, and a click that **reopens the panel**. So the predicted pixel move (`rounded-md` → pill, `text-on-surface-low` → `text-on-surface`) is confirmed — and worth recording: because `ghost` is transparent at rest, **the new radius is invisible until hover**, which makes this the mildest of the seven moves rather than the boldest.
  - **What I could NOT drive, stated plainly.** The other five migrated controls need cockpit state this environment cannot reach without a real provider-backed worker: the autopilot toggle and "Follow the worker" render only **after the project is launched** (measured: the Tasks rail says "Tasks appear here once the project is launched"), the tab-close `IconButton` needs an open file tab against a bound workspace, and the two `TextLink` disclosures need >N files / multiple cycles of findings. Their pixel outcomes are argued from the primitives' own measured docs and locked by the full suite, **not** from a screenshot — and the autopilot toggle in particular is asserted, not seen. `:2938`'s `FieldError` likewise needs a failing commit-diff fetch inside a real git workspace. Anyone re-opening this should drive those five before trusting the pixels.

### 2026-08-21 — DSC-13 DONE (the shared windowing primitive, adopted at five long-list surfaces — and SM-3's deferred measurement finally taken)

- **The measurement SM-3 asked for, taken first.** SM-3 deferred windowing "as speculative until a real jank profile exists on a large fixture". That profile now exists. Real gateway on **port 10777**, home **`/private/tmp/dsc13-wt/.dev-home`** (both asserted from its `PERSONALCLAW_READY` line before any number was believed — a foreign gateway was known to be on 10698 writing the real home), over a real populated store: **5,000 chat-session `.jsonl` files + 5,000 knowledge rows** seeded through the real stores. Real built SPA, headless Chromium 1280×900, **4× CPU throttle** (the Lighthouse convention). N varied by re-limiting the gateway's own 2.3 MB response, so every row is real data.

  | rows | DOM rows | DOM nodes | keystroke→paint | frame median | frame worst | nav→last row |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 100 | 100 | 4,188 | 13.1 ms | 8.4 ms | 10.4 ms | 1,434 ms |
  | 250 | 250 | 9,438 | 12.8 ms | 11.0 ms | 16.7 ms | 1,551 ms |
  | 500 | 500 | 18,188 | 21.4 ms | **21.4 ms** | 29.2 ms | 2,007 ms |
  | 1,000 | 1,000 | 35,688 | 36.6 ms | 19.2 ms | 45.8 ms | 3,008 ms |
  | 2,000 | 2,000 | 70,688 | 58.1 ms | 73.6 ms | 85.9 ms | 5,277 ms |
  | 5,000 | 5,000 | 175,688 | **137.0 ms** | 188.2 ms | 325.0 ms | 12,240 ms |

  **The number: interaction degrades at 500 rows** — the first N where the MEDIAN frame during a sustained scroll exceeds one 16.7 ms frame, i.e. where scrolling stops holding 60 Hz rather than merely dropping the odd frame. First measurable frame loss is at 250 (worst frame exactly 16.7 ms); it collapses to 13 fps at 2,000 and 5 fps at 5,000, and keystroke latency in the list's own search field crosses the 100 ms "instant" bound at 5,000. DOM nodes are linear at ~35/row, so nothing self-limits. Recorded in `web/src/design/windowedListAdoption.baseline.json`, beside the adoption count, so DSC-3's ratchet idiom can hold the line.

- **AFTER: every column flat in N.** 18 rendered rows and 1,337 nodes whatever the collection holds; keystroke 137 ms → **13.3 ms**; time-to-last-row 12,240 ms → **1,335 ms**; frame median 188 ms → 27 ms at 5,000 rows. **The honest trade, recorded rather than buried:** the windowed frame cost is *flat* at ~27 ms median under the 4× throttle, which is WORSE than the un-windowed list below ~500 rows (8.4 ms at 100) and better above it — the crossover sits between 500 and 1,000. **Unthrottled it costs nothing measurable: 8.3 ms median flat, byte-identical to the un-windowed 100-row baseline.** So the property bought is that the cost is BOUNDED, not that it is zero.
- **Threshold 64, chosen against the measurement and against inertness.** At or below 64 rows the primitive renders every row and emits no padding, no `aria-describedby` and no hint — byte-identical to the `<div>` it replaces, which is why adopting it moves no visual baseline. 64 is a ~4× margin under the 250-row first-frame-loss point. It is deliberately NOT set at the 500-row crossover: the list endpoints at knowledge and runs cap `limit` at **100** server-side, so any threshold above 100 would have made two of the five adoptions **inert controls** — shipped, counted, and never once engaged.
- **Adopted at five surfaces, `done_when`'s five, each with its own reason.** `pages/ChatPage.tsx` (sessions — **the one list that is uncapped at BOTH ends**: `/api/chat/sessions` has no server page size and the client applies no slice, and it really does reach 5,000 rows; windowed **per folder group**, which composes — each group observes the shared scroller and windows its own rows, so a 5,000-chat ungrouped bucket windows while a 3-chat folder passes through) · `pages/settings/DiagnosticsPanel.tsx` (logs — a 2,000-line ring buffer that fills without the user doing anything) · `pages/inbox/InboxPage.tsx` (uncapped client-side) · `pages/knowledge/KnowledgeListPage.tsx` and `pages/workflows/WorkflowsListPage.tsx` (server page size 100 today, so the window engages on a full first page). Held by an INVERSE ratchet — `design/windowedListAdoption.test.ts` asserts the count may only **grow** and asserts the five surfaces **by name**, because a bare total can be propped up by five adoptions at surfaces nobody's list ever gets long in.
- **Not adopted, with reasons.** The chat **board** view (`TagBoard`) reuses the same row through per-tag columns, each with its own scroller — five short columns, and the primitive composes there whenever a column gets long, so it was left for the surface that needs it. `pages/settings/AuditPanel.tsx` already has real cursor pagination (`PAGE_SIZE = 50` + "Load older events"), which solves the same problem a different way; converging those two is a product decision, not this atom. The tool-output line renderers (`toolRenderers/primitives.tsx` `LogView`, `native.tsx` hit-lists, `FilePreviews`) are already capped with a `MoreRow` disclosure. **`WorkflowRunDetail`'s step tree is the one genuine gap I am leaving open**: it is uncapped and roleless, but it is a *collapsible tree* with depth-indented rows, and windowing a tree needs a flattened-visible-rows contract the primitive does not have yet.
- **Each preserved behaviour is asserted, not claimed** (`ui/windowedList.test.tsx`, 19 tests). **Keyboard reach:** the primitive owns Arrow/Home/End/PageUp/PageDown and `reveal()` scrolls + re-windows *around* the target before focusing it, so `End` focuses row 5,000 that was never rendered and `Home` from the bottom focuses row 1 — and it re-windows around the target rather than stretching the range, so reaching row 4,000 mounts ~18 rows, not 4,009. **Focus survival:** a row that unmounts under the user parks focus on the named container and gets it back on scroll-back; measured before the fix, `document.activeElement === document.body`. **This one cannot be done from a blur handler** — removing a focused element does not reliably fire a `focusout` React can see (it does not in jsdom at all), so the handler that looked right never ran; it is checked from a layout effect keyed on the range instead. **Count semantics:** `aria-setsize` is ALWAYS `items.length` and `aria-posinset` the row's position in the whole list, verified in a real browser at `#/knowledge` — 16 of 100 rendered, `aria-setsize="100"`, `aria-posinset="1"`, 7,056 px of padding reserving the rest. **Deep link:** `anchorKey` mounts, scrolls to and focuses a row at any index; at inbox this **fixes a defect found on the way past** — `#/inbox?open=<id>` opened the panel for a row 400 deep and never scrolled the list to it. **`role="list"` has only `listitem` children**, because the un-rendered rows are container *padding*, not spacer elements — a spacer `<div>` would be an `aria-required-children` violation.
- **Find-in-page: cannot be preserved, so it is STATED.** Ctrl+F searches the DOM and the whole point is that most rows are not in it. `findHint` is a **required** prop, each adopter names its own search field, and the sentence is announced with the TRUE total ("Showing 16 of 100 items at a time so the list stays fast. Browser find only searches what is on screen — use the Search knowledge field above…"). A rail asserts every adopter's hint is substantive rather than an empty string.
- **Row heights: variable is SUPPORTED and every surface DECLARES.** All five declare `rowHeights="variable"`; none claims uniform. That is measured, not assumed — a real browser over the real store gives knowledge rows **34–76 px (median 76)** and session rows 66 px, `ListRow` carries no `h-*` class, its padding is `calc(16px * var(--space-scale))` with density presets at 0.8 and 0.68, and a log line is `whitespace-pre-wrap break-words` so its height is content- *and* width-dependent. A `ResizeObserver` measures each mounted row and replaces the estimate; `uniform` skips measurement entirely and observes no row.
- **A perf defect in my own first draft, found by measuring rather than by reading.** With measured heights in React state, `attachRow` and the row observer both depended on it, so every scroll re-created the ref callback for all 18 rendered rows and React detached + re-attached (and the observer re-observed) each one. Heights moved to a **ref with a version counter**, the scroll subscription moved behind a `recomputeRef` so it installs once, and scroll handling is **coalesced to one recompute per animation frame**.
- **A metric that was WRONG, discarded rather than reported.** The first scroll metric was wheel→next-two-frames. For an un-windowed list that is nearly free (no scroll handler runs at all); for a windowed one it always costs one extra frame, because deferring the recompute to the next frame is the design. It reported windowing as a 12.7 ms → 41 ms *regression at 100 rows* while the windowed list was in fact rendering 18 rows instead of 5,000. Replaced with the worst/median gap between frames during a sustained 30-frame scroll, which is what a user actually feels.
- **🪤 THE STALE BUNDLE TRAP, hit for real.** The first "after" run came back **byte-identical** to the before — 5,000 rows, 175,683 nodes, same latencies. Cause: the gateway resolves its SPA from `Path(personalclaw.__file__)/../static/dist`, and the venv on PATH is the **MAIN checkout's** editable install — so `personalclaw gateway` was serving the main tree's bundle and never saw this worktree's build. The provenance check passed because the HOME was right and the CODE was not. Fixed by serving this worktree's `web/dist` from the harness and **asserting the windowing marker is PRESENT/ABSENT as expected before measuring anything**; the before was then re-measured with the marker asserted ABSENT, so the pair is a real contrast and not two runs of one build. The lesson generalizes: asserting the port and the home does not assert the code.
- **Falsification — every mutation applied to the live line, re-read to confirm it landed, red observed, restored from a file copy (never `git checkout`).** (a) `start = 0; end = total` (window renders everything) → **9 red**, headed by the perf clause: *"expected 5000 to be less than or equal to 26"*. (b) `reveal(next, true)` → `focusRow(keys[next])` in the key handler → **3 red**: End/Home/ArrowDown all stop at the window edge. (c) `aria-setsize={end - start}` → **red**: *"expected '16' to be '5000'"* — the "20 of 5,000" regression, caught. (d) anchor effect made a no-op → **red**: *"expected [ 'r0' … ] to include 'r4000'"*. (e) focus parking removed → **red**: activeElement is `<body>`.
- **Visual baselines: MEASURED as pre-existing drift, deliberately NOT regenerated.** The DSC-2 harness reports **22 snapshots failing** — but they fail **byte-for-byte identically with and without this change** (control run on a pre-adoption build: `skills-dark` 25,068 = 25,068, `skills-light` 27,970 = 27,970, `tasks` 11,426/11,315 identical, `tools-light` 36,382 identical, `tools-dark` 33,712 vs 33,710 = 2 px of AA noise). Ten of the failing routes (tasks, tools, skills, triggers, agents, prompts, apps, projects, artifacts, learning) have no relationship to this diff at all. The baselines were last regenerated on **2026-08-09 (PR #966)**; this is twelve days of `main` drift, and regenerating 22 goldens here would sweep someone else's undocumented visual change into this commit. The other **6 failures are MISSING baselines, not drift**: `origin/main` tracks **32** goldens but the route manifest has since grown, so `artifacts`, `learning` and `knowledge?view=graph` have none in either theme (`git ls-files` confirms; `knowledge-light-darwin.png` and `tools-light-darwin.png` are tracked, those three are not). Playwright wrote them and failed, as it does for a first capture. Their accidental upside is the cleanest evidence in this cycle: the **pre-adoption** build was then compared against those **post-adoption**-generated goldens and passed (exit 0), so before and after are pixel-identical on all three. The generated PNGs were **deleted, not committed** — adding goldens for three surfaces this atom never touched would bless whatever they happen to look like today. So `main`'s 28 red visual tests are 22 drift + 6 missing, and this change owns none of them. **`#/knowledge` itself passes** — the direct evidence that adopting the primitive moves no pixels on an empty store, which is what the ≤64 passthrough is for. **axe: 32/32 clean, unchanged.**
- **Gate (from the repo root, venv on PATH).** `make lint` exit 0 (black 1,869 files, isort, flake8, mypy 943 sources) · `npm run typecheck:web` clean · **FULL `npm run test:web`: 458 files / 4,786 tests, 0 failures** · `npm run build --workspace web` clean · `make test` full Python suite · `npx playwright test` (DSC-2 harness) as recorded above. **`ui/disabledReasonCensus.test.ts` went red on its own** and was re-keyed in the same commit: it pins sites by `file:line`, and the knowledge adoption shifted `KnowledgeListPage.tsx:899` → `:917`. A path-scoped vitest run would not have seen it — which is exactly why the full suite is the gate. `docs/design/consistency-audit.json` regenerates on every full web run and drifts on `main` independently; left uncommitted.

### 2026-08-21 — DSC-14 (one data-fetching layer: no stale-then-flicker, one cache)
- **DONE (DSC-14).** `web/src/lib/useCachedData.ts` is **DELETED**. Its replacement is `web/src/lib/data/` — `store.ts` (the one cache: entries with an age, an in-flight dedup table, per-key subscribers, epochs), `keys.ts` (39 declared namespaces × a freshness window), `useQuery.ts`, `useMutation.ts`, `index.ts`. **142 files changed, +2068/−861.**
- **Census, taken on the branch point, not from the clause.** `grep -rl useCachedData web/src` → **124 files** (the clause says 123 at authoring, so it grew by one); **214 call sites**; 90 of the 124 import it, the rest name it in prose. Across the whole repo including docs, 136 files. After the migration: **0** call sites anywhere (§1 of the new ratchet), 100 files reference `useQuery` in comment-stripped code, 71 of them production modules.
- **The layer's API.** `useQuery(key, fetcher, { persist?, staleAfterMs? }) → { data, loading, revalidating, stale, error, status, refresh }` · `useMutation({ run, invalidates, onSuccess?, onError? }) → { mutate, pending, error }` · `invalidateKeys(keyOrPrefix, prefix?)` · `peekQuery` (**fresh-only**) / `peekEntry` (value + age) / `writeQuery` · `resetDataStore()` (test-only). The return shape is a strict SUPERSET of the old hook's, which is what made a 214-site migration a codemod plus judgement rather than 214 rewrites.
- **Three facts that used to be two.** `loading` = nothing to show → skeleton. `revalidating` = a request is on the wire → spinner. **`stale` = what IS on screen is past its window → LABEL it.** The old hook had grown `revalidating` precisely because `loading` could not express the second; `stale` is the third distinction it never grew, and it is the one the user sees.
- **Keys/staleness/invalidation are DECLARED.** `keys.ts` maps each namespace (the segment before the first `:`) to one of three policies — `LIVE` 5 s, `COLLECTION` 30 s, `CONFIG` 2 min — each with a one-line `why`. An **undeclared** namespace gets `staleAfterMs: 0`, i.e. *always stale*, so the failure mode of forgetting is a permanent label rather than a silent unlabelled paint. **The invalidation map lives in the mutation's own object literal** (`invalidates: [{ prefix: 'skill-proposals' }, 'skills']`), never inferred from a bust written three lines below the write.
- **`{ prefix }` is the default advice, and it found a bug while being written.** Accepting a skill proposal installs a skill; the pre-existing code busted only the proposals collection, so `#/skills` kept listing the library without it. The declared map now says `['skill-proposals' prefix, 'skills']`.
- **THE UNFIXED BUILD, DRIVEN — the defect measured before the fix was asserted.** Two worktrees, two bundles (`index-BPjAhZDf.js` at `origin/main` vs `index-DG0wTPZi.js` here — hashes differ, so before ≢ after), two gateways on two isolated dev homes (`.dev-home` created and populated under each worktree; the served bundle hash was asserted on each port BEFORE measuring, because asserting the port and the home does not assert the code). Surface: `#/settings`, the Inbox bento tile, `cache:settings:inbox` (`persist: true`). Protocol: retention → 30, tile settles, retention → 7 **out of band via the API**, hard reload with `/api/inbox/settings` held 7 s.

  | | BEFORE (`origin/main`) | AFTER |
  |---|---|---|
  | first paint after reload | **`30 day retention`** | **`30 day retention`** |
  | `[data-stale="true"]` nodes | **0** | **2** |
  | "updating" copy present | **false** | **true** |
  | settled paint | `7 day retention` | `7 day retention` |
  | verdict | **stale paint UNLABELLED, then silently replaced** | stale paint **LABELLED**, then replaced |

  Screenshots: `/tmp/dsc14_{BEFORE,AFTER}_firstpaint.png` — the AFTER frame shows `⟳ Updating inbox…` in the tile header beside "30 day retention".
- **A CONTROL RUN CHANGED THE DESIGN.** The migrated build was measured *before* the label existed and came back **worse**: `settled = 30`. Cause — the first cut skipped the request entirely for a value the registry declared fresh, and the persisted record's own timestamp was trusted across a reload, so a `settings` value 20 s old was "fresh" for 2 minutes and never corrected. Two changes fell out of that measurement, both now load-bearing: **(1) a mount ALWAYS revalidates** (the window decides whether the paint may go *unlabelled*, not whether to ask the server — requests are cheap because they dedup, a wrong number is not); **(2) a value seeded from sessionStorage is `at: 0` — "present, not current" — however recently it was written**, because the app was not running when the server changed and nothing invalidated it.
- **Two OTHER caches, both found by looking rather than by grep, both converged.** `pages/ChatPage.tsx` kept a private `chat-detail:` sessionStorage store with its own reader/writer and **no age at all** — the literal hand-rolled shape, invisible to any census of the helper because it never called it. Now `chat:detail:<key>` in the shared store, reachable by `invalidateKeys('chat:', true)`, and seeded through **fresh-only `peekQuery`**: a transcript has nowhere sane to hang an "updating" label, so it is fresh or it falls through to the loading state. `pages/workflows/WorkflowsListPage.tsx` (a DSC-13 surface) hand-rolled `useState × 4 + Promise.all + a mount effect` and therefore had **no cache at all** — three `useQuery` reads now, per-read `error` preserved so a definitions failure is not announced on the Runs tab.
- **What did NOT migrate, with the count: ONE file.** `pages/files/filesData.ts`'s `useDirCache` — a per-**path** listing cache with hierarchical subtree invalidation, a 400-path bound, and per-path generation guards so a load that started before an invalidate cannot write back. The shared layer has none of those three and inventing them for one consumer is the wrong trade. It is **named in the ratchet** (§3) rather than quietly excluded, and the note records that it still paints an unlabelled stale listing — the same defect class, one surface wide. Seven other storage-writing modules are named as **UI preference stores** (nav collapsed, appearance, list view/sort/scope, terminal labels, files tab, open file tabs, canvas order), so a NEW storage writer has to be classified on purpose or §3 fails.
- **Ratchets — `web/src/lib/data/dataLayerAdoption.test.ts`, 16 assertions, every scan comment-stripped and every census carrying a VACUITY assertion.** §1 the deleted helper's four call forms and its import path are absent tree-wide, and the module file is gone. §2 every literal cache key's namespace is declared — **and keys passed by NAME are resolved**, which is not decoration: `learning:*` reaches the layer only through `proposalCache.ts`'s exported constants, so a literal-only census missed the whole namespace and it went undeclared until a test three files away reddened on the always-stale fallback. §2 also fails on a **declared namespace nobody uses**. §3 the hand-rolled remainder is EXACTLY the named list (a new one fails; removing one fails too, so the list cannot rot). §4 the stale label's adoption may only grow, its four named surfaces are named, and **nothing derives a freshness label from `loading` or `revalidating`**.
- **Adoption of the label went through two CHOKEPOINTS, not N call sites.** `ui/ListControls` gained `stale` (taking its noun from the `results.noun` the surface already declares — same reasoning as the loading-noun rail: the source of truth was in the file) and `pages/settings/bento.tsx`'s `BentoCard` gained `stale`. **24 of 28 settings tiles** now pass it (the 4 that do not read no server data: identity, appearance, log level, one static card), plus inbox, knowledge and workflows. New primitive `ui/StaleNotice.tsx` + `.doc.ts`; `announce={false}` on the bento path, honouring `BentoCard`'s own recorded finding that **22 tiles shimmer simultaneously** on a cold `#/settings` open, so 22 polite live regions would queue 22 announcements for one page load.
- **Contract tests — `web/src/lib/data/dataLayerContract.test.tsx`, 19 tests in four sections.** **Dedup is asserted by COUNT:** five components mounting one key → `toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1)`, all five paint the same bytes, a late mounter joins the in-flight promise, and the **289k-request shape is bounded** (a fetcher that is a fresh arrow every render must not re-trigger; renders must settle; `refresh` identity is asserted stable). **Invalidation:** the write is reflected in a mounted reader with no `refresh()` in the component at all, reaches a SIBLING key of the same collection, leaves a key outside the map alone, and **holds the paint** instead of blanking to a skeleton. **Freshness:** the deleted helper's persistence algorithm is reproduced inline to show the record carries no age and therefore *nothing can be labelled*, then the same scenario labelled. **Error≠empty:** an empty success and a failure are different `status` values, and `LoadError` vs `EmptyState` differ structurally (`role="alert"` + Retry vs neither).
- **Falsification — five mutations, each applied to the LIVE line, each re-read to confirm it landed, red observed, restored from a `cp` file copy (never `git checkout`).** (a) `invalidateSpecs` → `void specs` (the declared map dropped) → **2 red**: "the write is reflected in a MOUNTED reader…" and "reaches a SIBLING reader…". (b) dedup keyed on `key + Math.random()` (what keying it on the fetcher's identity amounts to at real call sites) → **4 red**, headed by "five components mounting one key at once produce ONE request". (c) the rejection swallowed into `writeQuery(key, [])` — i.e. `.catch(() => [])` — → **2 red** in §4: "the layer reports a rejection instead of resolving empty". (d) `_readSession` trusting the persisted `at` → **1 red**: "a value seeded from a previous page load is stale". (e) the whole unfixed BUILD driven in a browser, above. Post-restore `git diff --stat` showed only the generated audit JSON.
- **Two rails widened rather than weakened, both because the new mechanism is stronger than the shape they encoded.** `ui/loadErrorState.test.tsx`'s error-branch-reachability proof accepted only "error branch precedes the loading branch" or "`finally { setLoading(false) }`"; `#/workflows` now hand-rolls neither, because `useQuery`'s `loading` falls false on a rejection **by construction** — asserted directly in the contract test, not inferred from source shape. `lib/siblingCacheStaleness.test.ts`'s prefix-mode probe was re-pointed from the deleted module to `lib/data/store.ts`.
- **Two contract changes recorded as DEVIATIONS, both deliberate.** (1) A revalidation over a cached value no longer reports `loading: true`. The old hook set `loading` from "nothing is cached" *while separately* holding the value on screen, so `loading` meant two different things depending on whether a value happened to be held; the three facts are now separate and `dataLayer.test.ts`'s "still reports loading during a refresh" became "a revalidation reports revalidating + stale, NOT loading". (2) `refreshAfterDecision` no longer needs the swept facet's next mount: `proposalCache.test.tsx`'s "the entry is GONE, so its next mount refetches" became "the swept facet re-read itself, unprompted" — the stronger property, asserted by call count.
- **A test-isolation finding worth its own line.** The layer's cache is a module singleton, which was already true of the helper but harmless there because it re-fetched on every mount. The first full run went **22 red across seven files this change never touched** (desktopCapabilities alone: 7, all 5 s timeouts) — leaked entries meant the next test's mount never fetched and it waited out its `waitFor` on the previous test's data. `web/src/test/setup.ts` now calls `resetDataStore()` in `afterEach` (imported by its **concrete** path, so the eight files that `vi.mock('../../lib/data')` wholesale cannot replace it with a factory that lacks the export). That took 22 → 5. `resetDataStore` also sweeps **every** `cache:`-prefixed sessionStorage row, not just the ones its own module instance wrote: `savingsByCompressor.test.tsx` calls `vi.resetModules()` and re-imports, so the fresh copy's `_persisted` set is empty while the previous copy's rows are still there — it painted case 1's numbers in case 3, twice.
- **🪤 A JSX opening tag cannot be found by scanning to the first `>`.** The scripted pass that wired `stale` through 23 bento tiles inserted the prop at the first `>` after `<BentoCard`, which lands **inside** `onClick={() => go('inbox')}`. Ten `TS1109`s. Re-run with a brace/quote-depth scan; the file was restored from a `cp` copy first, not patched forward.
- **🪤 An identifier codemod rewrites PATH STRINGS too.** `useCachedData` → `useQuery` turned `codeOf('lib/useCachedData.ts')` into `codeOf('lib/useQuery.ts')` (a test then failed on ENOENT rather than on its assertion) and rewrote a prose reference in `ui/motion/Entrance.tsx`. Both swept afterwards by grepping the new name in string position.
- **Gate (from the worktree root, main checkout's venv on PATH).** `npm ci` · `npm run typecheck:web` clean · **FULL `npm run test:web`: 462 files / 4,840 tests, 0 failures** · `npm run build --workspace web` clean · `make lint` (Python) exit 0 · **`make test` SKIPPED, with the reason: this change touches zero Python files** (`git show --stat HEAD` has no `.py`, no `Makefile`, no `pyproject.toml`). `ui/disabledReasonCensus.test.ts` went red on its own and was re-keyed in the same commit — it pins sites by `file:line` and the knowledge `stale` prop shifted `KnowledgeListPage.tsx:917` → `:918`, exactly as DSC-13 predicted. **No baseline needed regenerating:** `primitiveAdoption.baseline.json` and `windowedListAdoption.baseline.json` both stayed green (the migration moved imports and destructures, not raw controls or windowing adoption), and regenerating either without a red would have been a blind edit. `docs/design/consistency-audit.json` regenerates on every full web run and drifts on `main` independently; left uncommitted.

- **2026-08-22 — the primitive-adoption ratchet had SLACK, so its own stated guarantee was false for
  form elements. Tightened `rawInput` 149 → 147.** `primitiveAdoption.baseline.json`'s comment says
  *"a NEW bespoke element turns CI red"* and *"The numbers may only shrink"*. Measured against the
  regenerated audit: `rawButton` 265 vs baseline 265 (**zero slack, working as designed**), `rawDialog`
  0 vs 0, but **`rawInput` 147 vs baseline 149 — two units of slack**. Two new raw
  `<input>`/`<textarea>`/`<select>` could be added anywhere in the app with CI green.
  **Not hypothetical — it had already absorbed one.** `pages/settings/ProjectionRulesPanel.tsx` went
  from `5 raw-input` to `6` (audit score 9 → 10) and CI stayed green, because the slack covered it.
  That is the drift this ratchet exists to catch, landing unnoticed.
  **Falsified in BOTH directions, on a real `<input>` site:** with the baseline at **147**, adding one
  raw input reds — *"New bespoke form element(s) detected (148 > 147)"*. With the same probe in place
  and the baseline back at **149**, the suite is **5 passed**. So the slack was load-bearing, not
  cosmetic, and the tightening restores the guarantee the file claims.
  **A vacuous probe caught first, worth recording.** The initial falsification targeted
  `settings/PromptsPanel.tsx` because the audit credits it with `raw-input: 1` — but that file contains
  **no `<input`** at all (its one hit is a `<select>`/`<textarea>`), so the edit asserted, mutated
  nothing, and the run reported "5 passed" against an UNMUTATED tree. A green falsification proves
  nothing until the probe is confirmed present; the count was checked (`grep -c` → 0) rather than the
  pass being trusted. Re-run against `ProjectionRulesPanel.tsx`, which has four real `<input`s.
  **A `--root web` invocation also read as a pass** — `npx vitest run <path> --root web` reports
  *"Tests no tests"*, an UNRUN leg. Run from `web/`.
  **Recorded, not fixed: the audit artifact is inherently dirty-on-commit.**
  `docs/design/consistency-audit.json` is written by `web/src/design/consistencyAudit.test.ts`
  (a reporter that "never fails on drift") and carries a `generatedAt` timestamp, so **every**
  `npm run test:web` leaves the tree modified. That is why the committed copy reads stale (its
  `filesScanned` was 527 against a 547-file tree) and why passing sessions keep restoring it as stray
  drift. Refreshing it would re-drift on the next test run, so the file is deliberately left at `HEAD`
  here; the real fix is a design change to the artifact (drop the timestamp, or write only under an
  explicit flag), which is a separate call.
- **2026-08-22 — `consistency-audit.json` is now DETERMINISTIC, so the artifact can finally be kept
  current.** The committed audit carried a `generatedAt: new Date().toISOString()`, so **every**
  `npm run test:web` rewrote it. The consequence is written across three plans in this repo's own logs:
  the diff was discarded as noise at least **seven** times — `PERSONALITY-THEMES` ×3 (*"regenerated to
  a `generatedAt`-only delta and reverted rather than committed as noise"*, *"pure timestamp churn,
  discarded, and still stale on `main`"*), this plan ×3 (*"timestamp-only … reverted, kept out of the
  commit"*), and `SESSION-MANAGEMENT` ×1 — plus three more restores in the 2026-08-22 session before
  the cause was traced.
  **That churn is why the file went stale, and the staleness compounded.** Nobody commits a refresh
  when a real data delta cannot be told apart from a timestamp, so `filesScanned` drifted
  **310 → 442 → 518 → 520 → 523 → 527** across the logs while the tree grew to **547**. The record
  meant to expose drift was itself the most drifted file.
  **Fix: drop the timestamp.** Nothing read it — the only occurrence was the line that wrote it, and
  `consistency-audit.md` cites the *generator*, not a date. Git already records when a file changed. A
  committed generated artifact has to be a pure function of the tree it scans, or it cannot be
  committed at all.
  **Verified by running it three times:** with the timestamp gone, runs 2 and 3 produce a
  **byte-identical** file (`cmp` clean), so a suite run now leaves the tree clean.
  **Falsified:** with `generatedAt` put back, two consecutive runs differ by exactly that one line
  (`"generatedAt": "…12:52:38.345Z"` vs `"…12:53:26.704Z"`) and nothing else — the timestamp was the
  whole cause, not a symptom.
  **The refresh rides along, now that it is meaningful:** `filesScanned` 527 → 547,
  `ProjectionRulesPanel.tsx` `5 raw-input` → `6` (score 9 → 10), `outlineNoneCount` 141 → 142 over
  67 files, `reducedMotionFiles` 12 → 13, `animatedFiles` 167 → 168. `totals.driftHits` stays 8 and
  `filesWithDrift` stays 7 — the tree grew by 20 files without adding a NEW drifting file.
  **Related, shipped separately (#1889):** that same regenerated data is what exposed the
  `primitiveAdoption` ratchet's two units of `rawInput` slack. The two changes are independent; this
  one is why the measurement was available to make.
